## chest-pain
- Sources read: AHA-FA 6.4; ARC-LAY Heart Attack; MLP Heart attack first aid.
- Numbers checked: 162 to 325 mg, chew and swallow, alert adults, non-traumatic chest pain — AHA-FA 6.4 (2b, B-NR); exclusions known aspirin allergy, advised by a health care professional not to take it (recommendation), "bleeding risk" (background); if uncertain wait (2b, C-EO).
- Numbers checked: two to four 81 mg tablets (162 to 324 mg) or one 325 mg tablet — ARC-LAY; conditions awake, understands simple commands, can chew or swallow, allowed aspirin.
- Numbers checked: median 1.6 hours vs 3.5 hours — early vs late aspirin — AHA-FA 6.4 supportive text.
- Numbers checked: about 1 in 300 — cardiac arrest on the way by private vehicle — AHA-FA 6.4.
- Numbers checked: longer than 3 to 5 minutes — persistent chest pain — ARC-LAY; within the first 2 hours — most heart attack deaths — ARC-LAY.
- Notes: atypical signs from AHA-FA (women, diabetes, older adults: shortness of breath, back/neck/jaw/stomach pain, sweating, nausea, lightheadedness), ARC-LAY (women, diabetes, second heart attack) and MLP (mental status change in older adults; weakness or fatigue). Nitroglycerin: ARC-LAY "offer to assist" with prescribed nitroglycerin; MLP "help them take it"; no dose. MLP: aspirin only if instructed (open question). "Not ibuprofen or acetaminophen" rests on MLP's nothing-by-mouth-except-prescribed-heart-medicine. Off-grid walk-out guidance is labelled group judgement (open question).

## stroke
- Sources read: AHA-FA 6.2, 6.3, 6.7; ARC-LAY Stroke; ASA Aspirin and Stroke, Stroke Symptoms; MLP Stroke.
- Numbers checked: 4 1/2 hours — clot-dissolving treatment window from first symptoms — MLP.
- Numbers checked: more than 10 percent within 3 months — stroke after TIA — ARC-LAY.
- Numbers checked: below 70 mg/dL — hypoglycaemia — AHA-FA 6.7 (page prints "<70 or <2.8 mmol/L"; mmol/L omitted, open question).
- Numbers checked: under 1 year — children's stroke presenting as seizures — AHA-FA 6.3.
- Notes: AHA names FAST (and the Cincinnati scale); ARC-LAY, ASA and MLP also use FAST; none uses BE-FAST. FAST technique (smile, raise both arms, repeat a simple sentence) from MLP, confirmed word for word on re-opening. No aspirin: ASA. Last known well and recovery position (not fully awake, drooling, trouble swallowing): ARC-LAY. Glucose check if it does not delay EMS (2a) and hypoglycaemia as a common mimic: AHA-FA 6.2. Nothing by mouth is group judgement (open question).

## seizure
- Sources read: AHA-FA 6.6; ARC-LAY Seizures; CDC First Aid for Seizures.
- Numbers checked: 1 to 2 minutes — most seizures stop — AHA-FA 6.6.
- Numbers checked: more than 5 minutes — status epilepticus / emergency — AHA-FA 6.6, CDC, ARC-LAY (timer 300 s).
- Numbers checked: 5 to 10 minutes — not back to baseline after the seizure stops — AHA-FA 6.6 (timer 600 s, the upper end).
- Numbers checked: under 6 months — infant seizure needs EMS — AHA-FA 6.6.
- Numbers checked: up to 20 minutes — drowsy and disoriented afterwards — ARC-LAY.
- Notes: AHA classes: EMS list (1, C-LD); help to ground, recovery position, clear area (1); stay with them (1); no restraint (3: Harm); nothing in the mouth, no food, liquid or oral medicine during or while responsiveness reduced (3: Harm). CDC: side with mouth to the ground, soft flat thing under the head, glasses off, loosen neck, no mouth-to-mouth during the seizure, 911 list including diabetes with loss of consciousness, pregnancy, never had a seizure before. ARC-LAY: impossible to swallow one's own tongue; call if unsure of seizure history; medication only per training; CPR if not breathing after. Seizure-in-water technique is group judgement (open question).

## low-blood-sugar
- Sources read: AHA-FA 6.7 (and 6.2 for the stroke look-alike); ARC-LAY Diabetic Emergencies; CDC Treatment of Low Blood Sugar; CDC Low Blood Sugar; MLP Low blood sugar.
- Numbers checked: at least 20 g — oral glucose — AHA-FA 6.7 supportive text.
- Numbers checked: 10 minutes — no improvement means EMS — AHA-FA 6.7 (1, C-EO) (timer 600 s).
- Numbers checked: about 1 teaspoon — granulated sugar slurry under the tongue, awake children unwilling or unable to swallow — AHA-FA 6.7 (2b).
- Numbers checked: below 70 mg/dL — low blood sugar — AHA-FA 6.7, MLP, CDC repeat rule.
- Numbers checked: 7 ounces orange juice; 14 ounces milk; 20 to 25 Skittles; 2 strips of fruit rolls; 10 to 20 jellybeans — sources of 20 grams — ARC-LAY (wording "Good sources for 20 grams of sugar"). Millilitre conversions (about 200 ml, about 400 ml) are the app's.
- Numbers checked: 15 grams, wait 15 minutes, repeat while below 70 mg/dL — CDC 15-15 rule (used only as the source of "repeat"; open question); within 15 minutes — usual waking after glucagon — CDC (re-opened again after the restart).
- Notes: AHA: oral glucose if awake and can swallow (1); tablets preferred (2a); dietary sugars acceptable (2a); not for anyone not fully awake or unable to swallow (3: Harm). ARC-LAY: no diet drink; if unsure low or high, give sugar; assist with glucagon, trained people encouraged to give it; no dose. CDC: balanced snack with protein and carbs; crackers and cheese after glucagon; contact a doctor immediately after glucagon. MLP: signs list, do not drive.

## asthma
- Sources read: AHA-FA 6.1; ARC-LAY Asthma Attack; MLP Asthma.
- Numbers checked: 500 mL (about 16 oz) bottle and 150 mL (about 5 oz) paper cup — improvised spacers — AHA-FA 6.1 supportive text.
- Numbers checked: 5 to 10 seconds — breath hold after the puff — ARC-LAY.
- Numbers checked: 10 to 15 minutes after the first dose — second dose if signs continue and EMS not arrived — ARC-LAY (timer 600 s, the earliest point).
- Numbers checked: twice a week or more — reliever use that means asthma may not be controlled — MLP (re-opened again after the restart).
- Notes: AHA classes: help with own prescribed bronchodilator as needed (1, B-R); spacer or nebulizer preferred (2a); improvised spacer reasonable (2a). ARC-LAY technique (verify, shake, cap off, spacer, breathe out, lips sealed, press, slow deep breath, hold; several normal breaths from the spacer if needed); untrained: give it to them to use as directed by their provider; sit up straight; triggers list; asterisked emergency signs. MLP emergency signs: bluish lips and face, decreased alertness (severe drowsiness or confusion), extreme difficulty breathing, rapid pulse, severe anxiety, sweating, difficulty speaking, breathing temporarily stops. No puff count in any source (open question). Moving away from triggers is common sense, stated as such in the detail.

## overdose
- Sources read: AHA-SPEC 21.11 and 21.3; AHA-FA 8.1; ARC-LAY Opioid Overdose; MLP Naloxone Nasal Spray; CDC Lifesaving Naloxone, What to Do If You Think Someone Is Overdosing, naloxone fact sheet; NIDA Naloxone DrugFacts; cpr-adult.json (its naloxone sentence, for consistency).
- Numbers checked: every 2 to 3 minutes, alternate nostrils, new spray each time — repeat naloxone — MLP (timer 120 s).
- Numbers checked: within 2 to 3 minutes — naloxone can restore breathing — CDC Lifesaving Naloxone.
- Numbers checked: at least four hours — stay and watch if help has not arrived — CDC Lifesaving Naloxone (timer 14400 s).
- Numbers checked: 30 to 90 minutes — naloxone duration; another 2 hours after the last dose — monitoring — NIDA.
- Numbers checked: 72% — recurrent respiratory depression in one IV low-dose study — AHA-SPEC 21.11 (first reading only; not used in the protocol).
- Notes: AHA-SPEC 21.11 classes: lay rescuers CPR with breaths for unresponsive and not breathing normally (1, B-NR); lay and trained rescuers should not delay standard resuscitation or activating EMS (1, C-EO); naloxone in cardiac arrest provided it does not interfere with standard resuscitation (2b); trained rescuers give breaths with a definite pulse (1, B-NR); observe in a health care setting (1, C-LD); repeat doses for recurrence (2a); nonopioid sedatives not reversed; recurrence if the opioid outlasts the antagonist. 21.3: naloxone first in combined opioid-benzodiazepine poisoning (2a, B-NR). AHA-FA 8.1: breathing normally does not need naloxone; intranasal takes several minutes; unlikely to harm. MLP technique: on the back, neck supported, head tilted back; do not prime; nozzle in one nostril until fingers touch the nose; press firmly; call 911; recovery position; another dose if not waking or breathing normally or relapses. CDC: signs list, treat as overdose if unsure, keep awake and breathing, on the side, stay, Good Samaritan laws, will not harm with other drugs. NIDA withdrawal: headaches, blood pressure changes, rapid heart rate, sweating, nausea, vomiting, tremors; usually not life threatening. Consistent with cpr-adult s5 (naloxone only if it does not interrupt CPR). Written without moral comment.

## fainting
- Sources read: AHA-FA 6.8, 5.1, 5.2; ARC-LAY Fainting; MLP Fainting.
- Numbers checked: 1 to 2 minutes — no improvement means EMS — AHA-FA 6.8 (2b) and ARC-LAY (timer 120 s; ARC-LAY re-opened again after the restart).
- Numbers checked: about 30% — injuries among ED patients after syncope — AHA-FA 6.8; about 50% — reduction in syncope with counterpressure manoeuvres — AHA-FA 6.8; at least 2 minutes — continuous manoeuvres beneficial — AHA-FA 6.8.
- Numbers checked: 6 to 12 inches — raise feet, no trauma, e.g. simple fainting — AHA-FA 5.2 (2b); the cm conversion (15 to 30 cm) is the app's. About 12 inches (30 cm) — MLP.
- Numbers checked: at least 10 to 15 minutes lying down — MLP (timer 600 s). About a minute — comes to — ARC-LAY. Over age 50 and "within a couple of minutes" — MLP 911 list.
- Notes: AHA classes: safe position, assisted sitting or lying (1); counterpressure manoeuvres once safe (2a); lower body preferred (2b); not with heart attack or stroke symptoms (3: Harm); for vasovagal or orthostatic presyncope. ARC-LAY: lie on back; cross legs and/or contract leg muscles; recovery position if breathing, unresponsive and uninjured; CPR if not breathing. MLP: side if vomited, head between knees if lying is impossible, get up slowly, provider for first faint, frequent faints or new symptoms.

## vomiting-diarrhea
- Sources read: MLP Nausea and vomiting - adults, Diarrhea, Abdominal pain, Appendicitis; ARC-LAY Dehydration and Poison Exposure; CDC Preventing Norovirus; CDC How to Make Water Safe in an Emergency; CDC Yellow Book Travelers' Diarrhea; dehydration.json (rehydration is sent there, not restated).
- Numbers checked: longer than 24 hours; unable to keep fluids down 12 hours or more; no urine 8 or more hours; 3 or more vomits in 1 day — MLP 003117.
- Numbers checked: fever above 101°F (38.33°C; 100.4°F, 38°C in children); 5 days adult, 2 days child — MLP 003126. App shows 38.3°C.
- Numbers checked: 12 to 24 hours — appendicitis pain moves to the lower right — MLP 000256.
- Numbers checked: at least 20 seconds handwashing; at least 2 days (48 hours) after symptoms stop; 1,000 to 5,000 ppm = 5 to 25 tablespoons of household bleach (5% to 8%) per gallon; at least 5 minutes contact — CDC Preventing Norovirus.
- Numbers checked: rolling boil 1 minute, 3 minutes above 6,500 feet; 8 drops per gallon of 5% to 9% bleach; stand at least 30 minutes — CDC water page.
- Task 13 fix round 1 (2026-11-17, controller ruling): altitude for the 3-minute boil changed from above 6,500 feet (CDC) to above 5,000 feet (EPA Emergency Disinfection of Drinking Water: "At altitudes above 5,000 feet (1,000 meters), boil water for three minutes"), in s8 detail and the final checklist line, so the protocol agrees with surv-make-water-safe. EPA source added; reviewed stays 2026-11-17.
- Numbers checked: at least 60% alcohol hand sanitizer; loperamide "rarely given to children aged <6 years"; BSS not under 3 or in pregnancy, not generally under 12, caution up to 18 with viral infections — CDC Yellow Book.
- Notes: ARC-LAY Dehydration asterisked signs (shock, no tears, vomiting or diarrhoea more than a few days, unable to keep fluids down, little or no urine, bloody or black stool, sharp or persistent belly pain, sweet breath, sunken eyes/cheeks, tented skin). ARC-LAY Poison Exposure: multiple people affected marked for immediate emergency treatment. MLP Abdominal pain: sudden sharp pain, tender or rigid belly, blood in vomit or stool → help right away; no medicine without a provider; sips for mild pain; avoid solids first few hours. MLP Diarrhea: OTC anti-diarrhoeals unless fever or bloody diarrhoea. CDC Yellow Book: antimotility agents alone not for bloody diarrhoea or fever; BSS contraindications; black tongue and stool; sodas in quantity cause osmotic diarrhoea; "boil it, cook it, peel it, or forget it". No dose of any medicine is given (open question).

## Task 14 path check
Every path through each protocol was listed with a script (chest-pain 6, stroke 5, seizure 14, low-blood-sugar 9, asthma 6, overdose 30 including the naloxone re-dose and relapse loops, fainting 8, vomiting-diarrhea 8), and every cross-reference was checked against the exact titles in src/protocols/. Every protocol screens unresponsive-and-not-breathing-normally first (seizure does it after the seizure ends, since breathing is abnormal during one; overdose sends that case to CPR with breaths, naloxone not delaying it). Changes made by the check:
- chest-pain s7 (own nitroglycerin): added the warning "only if fully alert and can swallow", because the aspirin-exclusion branch (which includes not fully alert) skips straight to it.
- stroke s5: "nothing by mouth" contradicted s7, which sends a measured low blood sugar to the Low blood sugar protocol; s5 now names that single exception, labelled group judgement, with the open question.
- seizure s5: the "still seizing" question looped to itself; it now ends in s13 (keep protecting, evacuate, what to do when it stops).
- seizure s7: "roll them onto their side" applied to people found already fully awake; now "if they are not fully awake".
- low-blood-sugar s8: the question joined two conditions; now "Are they still fully awake?" with swallowing in s9's action and warning.
- asthma s7: answering Yes early would have given the second dose before Red Cross's 10 to 15 minutes; the detail now says to start evacuating at once for life-threatening signs and keep the timer for the second dose.
- fainting: cross-references to two protocols reworded so that titles containing "or" are not ambiguous ("the Chest pain or heart attack protocol, or the Stroke protocol").
Every by-mouth step carries its exclusions in the action or warning: chest-pain s5/s6/s7, stroke s5/s7, seizure s7 (AHA harm), low-blood-sugar s3/s4/s5/s9/s21/s23, vomiting-diarrhea s7/s24/s25/s26.

## Task 14 summary-field re-read
After the path check, summary, notes, donts and evacuate of all eight were re-read against the steps. Changes: stroke summary, notes and second don't now carry the measured-low-sugar exception; vomiting-diarrhea s9 action and the evacuate list now follow Red Cross for "cannot keep fluids down" (no time limit; MedlinePlus 12 hours given beside it), and the first evacuate line sends a possible poison or fumes to the Poisoning and Carbon monoxide protocols instead of claiming its own urgency; overdose don't on the nasal spray changed from "test or prime" to "prime" (MedlinePlus says only "do not prime"). No other field disagreed with its steps.

## Task 14 review fixes (2026-11-17)
Independent review: task-14-content-review.md (1 Critical, 6 Important, 15 Minor plus M16 noted). Sources re-opened for the fixes on 2026-11-17: Red Cross Asthma Attack, Opioid Overdose, Fainting, Dehydration, Heart Attack; SAMHSA Opioid Overdose Prevention Toolkit 2013 (Connecticut DMHAS mirror, pages 8 and 9); AHA 2024 First Aid 6.2, 6.4, 6.7, 6.8; CDC Treatment of Low Blood Sugar; Michigan Medicine Seizure First Aid and Safety (revised 11/2019); MedlinePlus Nitroglycerin Sublingual (a601086, revised 2024-06-20), Viral gastroenteritis (000252, reviewed 2026-04-27), Abdominal pain (003120); CDC Yellow Book Travelers' Diarrhea.
- C1 asthma: s8 (second dose) now asks "After the second dose: are they clearly better?" Yes → s11 (easing); No → s10 (evacuate urgently, sitting up; MedlinePlus life-threatening signs folded into s10's detail). The old s9 screen was removed (no longer reachable). Red Cross: "Unimproved after medication*". Summary and evacuate line 1 aligned.
- I1 overdose: s11 now says start evacuating now (or wait for the rescue already called) and watch the whole time for at least 4 hours with naloxone ready; the timer supports the watch and restarts after each further dose. s13 is "keep going". Attribution: CDC "until emergency help arrives or for at least four hours"; SAMHSA "at least 4 hours from the last dose of naloxone"; NIDA 2 hours after the last dose. Summary and evacuate list aligned.
- I2 overdose: s11 warn and detail cover waking agitated (SAMHSA: withdrawal can feel unpleasant; some become agitated or combative and need help to remain calm; comfort them; medical care even if they revive and seem better), naloxone wearing off (NIDA 30 to 90 minutes; SAMHSA symptoms may return) and, as labelled group judgement, not letting them go off alone. SAMHSA toolkit added to sources; open question added.
- I3 fainting: anyone who actually passed out now gets the help wording (s8) and evacuation: s9 (MedlinePlus list) decides urgency, s11 (new) evacuates the rest (AHA 2024 6.8 Class 2b "if syncope occurs"; Red Cross asterisks loss of consciousness). Off-grid reality in s8 and s11 detail; open question asks about a narrower rule. Summary, notes and evacuate list aligned.
- I4 stroke and low-blood-sugar: one rule. Stroke s7 asks whether a meter reading is below 70 mg/dL; new s6b asks "Do they have diabetes treated with insulin or diabetes pills?"; new s6c gives at least 20 g of sugar with the Low blood sugar protocol's wording and its awake and swallow exclusions (AHA 6.7 Class 1 and Class 3 Harm; Red Cross "if you can't tell... give some sugar"), then keeps evacuating as a stroke. Stroke s5 no longer claims a source for nothing by mouth; other food, drink and medicine are withheld as labelled group judgement. Low-blood-sugar s2 now says the sugar step still applies with stroke signs. Red Cross Diabetic Emergencies added to stroke's sources. Open question rewritten.
- I5 vomiting-diarrhea: s9 leads with the Red Cross Dehydration asterisked list plus MedlinePlus's "headache or stiff neck" (restored to "or"); the MedlinePlus 24 h, 12 h, 8 h and 3-vomits figures and the Viral gastroenteritis list are information in the detail. Evacuate list and open question updated.
- I6 chest-pain: "under 18" added to the s5 check, the don't and the notes (AHA 6.4 "alert adults"); open question on the age.
- M1 chest-pain s7: warn "Only if fully alert. Have them sit or lie down first: nitroglycerin can make them dizzy or faint (MedlinePlus)"; MedlinePlus Nitroglycerin Sublingual cited.
- M2 chest-pain s9: "Get them to hospital now, without exertion: if a rescue or carry can reach you, keep them resting and wait; walking is the last option (see detail). Even if the pain eases."
- M3 low-blood-sugar s22: CDC "If they don't wake up within 15 minutes after the injection, they should receive one more dose", from their own kit as it directs; 15-minute timer (900 s).
- M4 seizure s9: "diabetes with loss of consciousness" (CDC wording).
- M5 seizure s30: Michigan Medicine technique (head above water, tilted back if possible; emergency department whether or not they seem to recover) cited; action, evacuate list and open question updated.
- M6 asthma s6: "press the canister firmly as they breathe in"; puffs as their own prescription or plan (Red Cross "Firmly press the inhaler canister").
- M7 asthma s7: the action now says "Blue lips, drowsy or cannot speak: evacuate urgently now (see detail)".
- M8 fainting: the MedlinePlus list (s9) is reached only by people who passed out; the felt-faint path ends at s10.
- M9 fainting s2: "If they have just gone down, watch their breathing for up to about a minute while they come round. Not breathing normally at any point: start CPR." 60 s timer; the question is "After about a minute: are they still not responding?" (Red Cross: comes to after about a minute may simply have fainted).
- M10 fainting s22: pointers to the Asthma attack and Anaphylaxis (severe allergic reaction) protocols added.
- M11 vomiting-diarrhea s25: nothing by mouth for vomiting blood or coffee-ground material (group judgement); added to the sips open question and the donts.
- M12 vomiting-diarrhea s5: belly pain with possible pregnancy and unable to pass stool (especially with vomiting) (MedlinePlus Abdominal pain), and stomach pain not going away after a bowel movement (MedlinePlus Diarrhea, in the detail).
- M13 vomiting-diarrhea s7: CDC wording "avoid administering BSS to children aged 18 or under with viral infections".
- M14 vomiting-diarrhea s8: "boil it, cook it, peel it, or forget it" given as the traditional rule that does not fully prevent illness; the unclear shortcuts sentence removed.
- M15 vomiting-diarrhea s2: "or have they had a seizure" added (as Dehydration). Fever thresholds differ (101°F MedlinePlus Diarrhea here; 102°F MedlinePlus Dehydration in dehydration.json): recorded as an open question.
- M16: nothing to change (72% figure not used).
- Reviewer question 8 (sports drinks): MedlinePlus Viral gastroenteritis re-read word for word (see the open question). s7 now lists oral rehydration solution first ("best for heavy diarrhoea", consistent with dehydration.json's CDC line) and gives the MedlinePlus wording in the detail; dehydration.json not edited.
- Could not verify items: Red Cross Heart Attack "Keep person from getting cold or overheated" confirmed under General Care on re-opening; MedlinePlus Appendicitis "12 to 24 hours" confirmed in this task's second pass ("This most often occurs 12 to 24 hours after the illness starts"); CDC What to Do If You Think Someone Is Overdosing and the American Stroke Association Stroke Symptoms page were opened in this task (see Task 14 source keys).
- Open questions added: every actual faint; feeling faint without passing out (AHA vs Red Cross asterisks); evacuating during the naloxone watch; revived person who wants to leave; sports drinks; two fever thresholds; aspirin age limit. Rewritten: sugar and nothing by mouth with stroke signs; seizure in water; fainting warning signs; which vomiting and diarrhoea triggers; sips with severe belly pain or blood in the vomit.
- Path check redone on all eight (chest-pain 6 paths, stroke 9, seizure 14, low-blood-sugar 9, asthma 6, overdose 30, fainting 7, vomiting-diarrhea 8); all cross-referenced titles exist. Changes made by this check: vomiting-diarrhea evacuate line reworded so two protocol titles are not joined by "or"; stroke s10 detail now asks the user to pass on any sugar given and when. The other paths were confirmed: no path gives anything by mouth to someone not fully awake; the asthma second-dose No branch and fainting passed-out branch both end in evacuation; overdose relapse loops return to the breathing check before any further dose.
- Summary fields re-read on all eight: asthma, overdose, fainting, stroke and vomiting-diarrhea summaries, notes, donts and evacuate lists were brought in line as listed above (stroke summary/notes/don't now give the sugar rule; overdose evacuate list starts at once; fainting summary/notes/evacuate now say anyone who passed out needs care; vomiting-diarrhea summary names the Red Cross list and the donts add vomiting blood). chest-pain don't and notes carry "under 18". low-blood-sugar and seizure fields already matched their steps.

## npa
- Sources read: TCCC26 (4, 5e, Jan 2024 summary); SKILLSETS; CLS-07-2020; CMC-NPA-2025; SP-NPA.
- Numbers checked:
  - 32 Fr — standard NPA, fits an average adult — CLS-07-2020 skill 3 and CMC-NPA-2025 (two documents).
  - 90 degrees to the face, bevel toward the septum, toward the ground — CLS-07-2020 and CMC-NPA-2025.
  - About 2 inches, then rotate the curve downward — left nostril — CLS-07-2020 and CMC-NPA-2025.
  - 1000 mL bag-valve-mask — TCCC26 5e (two openings).
  - 1 to 2 seconds per squeeze, every 5 to 6 seconds (10 to 12 a minute) — BVM — CLS-07-2020 skill 5 (first reading only; also in slides #TCCC-CLS-PPT-07).
  - 6 to 9 cm — adult NPA length — SP-NPA (two openings).
- Notes: Clear fluid from nose or ears contraindication: CLS-07-2020 speaker notes and slides (two documents). Absolute and relative contraindications and complications: SP-NPA (two openings). Indication difference between 2020 and 2024/2026: open question. No child sizing (TCCC gives none; StatPearls child sizes not used). No drug.

## needle-decompression
- Sources read: TCCC26 (5a, 6f); SKILLSETS; CPP-08-2022; CPP-NDC-2023; CLS-08-2020; SP-TPTX.
- Numbers checked:
  - 14-gauge or 10-gauge, 3.25-inch needle/catheter — TCCC26 5a (two openings), CPP-08-2022, CPP-NDC-2023, CLS-08-2020.
  - 5th intercostal space anterior axillary line; 2nd intercostal space mid-clavicular line; not medial to the nipple line — TCCC26 5a (quoted on second opening), CPP-08-2022, CPP-NDC-2023.
  - 90 degrees (perpendicular), just over the top of the lower rib, to the hub — TCCC26 5a quotes, CPP-08-2022, CPP-NDC-2023.
  - 5 to 10 seconds — hold — TCCC26 5a quote, CPP-08-2022, CPP-NDC-2023, CLS-08-2020.
  - Below 90% / 90% or greater — SpO2 sign and success — TCCC26 5a quotes; CPP sheets.
  - Below 8 or above 20 breaths a minute — respiratory distress — CPP-08-2022 and CPP-NDC-2023.
  - More than 8 cm — catheter length recommended — SP-TPTX (first reading only; complication note only).
- Notes: Indication list quoted from TCCC26 5a on the second opening. Second attempt at the other site, consider the other side, repeat at same site if symptoms recur, continue to circulation after a second failure, both sides in traumatic arrest: TCCC26 5a. Position supine or recovery unless conscious needing to sit up for maxillofacial trauma: TCCC26 5a. Finger thoracostomy / chest tube: TCCC26 6f (treating medical provider) and SKILLSETS (CP only); mentioned only as out of scope. Do not go through a chest seal: CLS-08-2020 slides. CPR position: as in march (existing open question). Timer 10 s (upper end of 5 to 10 s). Log text names the side (two insertion steps). Consistent with chest-wound s7 (burp first, then needle decompression for trained users).

## cricothyrotomy
- Sources read: TCCC26 (4, Summaries 2021 and Jan 2024); SKILLSETS; CMC-CRIC-2023 (open and bougie cards); CLS-07-2020 (BVM); SP-CRIC.
- Numbers checked:
  - Under 10 mm outer diameter, 6 to 7 mm internal diameter, 5 to 8 cm intratracheal length — cannula — TCCC26 4f (PDF summary and quote).
  - 1-inch vertical skin incision; half-inch horizontal membrane incision; no deeper than half an inch from the skin — both CMC-CRIC-2023 cards.
  - 10 mL — cuff inflation — both cards.
  - A quarter to 1 inch beyond the cuff — ET tube depth — both cards.
  - At least 10 seconds; below 8 or above 30 a minute; below 90% — breathing check and BVM triggers — both cards.
  - About 2 cm below the laryngeal prominence — membrane — SP-CRIC (two openings).
  - Below 5 to 12 years — relative contraindication — SP-CRIC (two openings). App uses 12.
  - Up to 50% — significant bleeding — SP-CRIC (two openings).
- Notes: Full stepwise technique with landmarks found (two CMC cards, consistent), so the file is fully written, not a draft. TCCC26 lists the bougie-aided technique first; the 2021 summary says use the technique you are best trained in; the protocol asks "Are you using a bougie?" and gives both. Lidocaine (TCCC26 4f) stated only as "medicines outside this app". Capnography and failure plan: open question.

## hemostatic-packing
- Sources read: TCCC26 (3); SKILLSETS; CLS-09-2024 skill instructions and notes; CLS-06-2020; CMC-06-2021; WMS-WOUND.
- Numbers checked:
  - At least 3 minutes — pressure — TCCC26 3b quote; CLS-09-2024; CLS-06-2020; CMC-06-2021.
  - 1 to 2 inches above the skin — packing — CLS-09-2024; CLS-06-2020 (quote on second opening).
  - 90 seconds — junctional packing standard and repacking after rebleed — CLS-06-2020 (quote); CLS-09-2024 notes.
  - 2 to 3 inches — tourniquet above the wound — TCCC26; CLS-09-2024 notes.
  - 6-inch elastic bandage, 3-inch tape, 1 to 1.5 times — neck wrap — CLS-06-2020 (first reading only).
  - 5 minutes — WMS minimum pressure for hemostatic agents — WMS-WOUND dtic copy (quote) and FEMECV copy.
- Notes: Combat Gauze first choice; Celox Gauze, ChitoGauze, XStat (deep narrow-tract junctional), iTClamp: TCCC26 3b quote. XStat not removed in field, pressure optional: TCCC26. Chest and belly wounds excluded to agree with bleeding s14/s15 and abdominal-wound's don't. Limb tourniquet first for life-threatening limb bleeding: TCCC26 3a.

## junctional-bleeding
- Sources read: TCCC26 (3b, 3c, Summary of 2021 Changes); SKILLSETS; CLS-06-2020; CMC-06-2021; CMC-PDD-2021.
- Numbers checked:
  - 90 seconds — packing and device standard — CLS-06-2020, CMC-06-2021 (quote), CMC-PDD-2021.
  - At least 3 minutes; 1 to 2 inches — as hemostatic-packing.
  - 20 full turns — CRoC, then release and reposition the disc — CMC-06-2021 (quote on second opening).
  - About 30 degrees — JETT pad angle — CMC-06-2021 (quote).
  - 90 degrees — arm out for an armpit wound — CLS-06-2020 (first reading only).
  - 6-inch bandage, 3-inch tape — neck — CLS-06-2020 (first reading only).
- Notes: CoTCCC recommends no junctional products (2021 summary); devices named from CMC-06-2021. Time written on forehead (SAM, CRoC) or the JETT loop handle: CMC-06-2021 quotes. Groin landmark differs between CLS-06-2020 and CMC-PDD-2021: both given (open question). No time limit for junctional devices in any source read (open question). Neck airway watch: TCCC26 3c.

## pelvic-binder
- Sources read: TCCC26 (6a); SKILLSETS; PC1602 (two openings); CPP-PCD-2025; CLS-09-2025; JTS-56; BSA-WFA.
- Numbers checked:
  - Greater trochanters (and pubic symphysis), not the anterior superior iliac spine — placement — PC1602 quotes; CPP-PCD-2025 (greater trochanters).
  - Indications list (severe blunt or blast plus pelvic pain, major leg amputation or near amputation, exam findings, unconsciousness, shock) — TCCC26 6a quote and PC1602 quote.
  - About 8 to 12 hours — reassess and loosen if stable — PC1602 quote.
- Notes: Ankles or feet loosely together, thighs if amputated, toes secured: PC1602 quote; legs together: CPP-PCD-2025 and BSA-WFA. No springing: PC1602 quote; avoid rocking: BSA-WFA. Improvised methods and the single cravat / linked tourniquets caution: PC1602 quotes. Reapply if shock appears or worsens: CPP-PCD-2025 (first reading only). Rigid litter: BSA-WFA. Fracture protocol s5/s6 (hip, pelvis, thigh: keep still, do not move unless you must) agrees.

## traction-splint (removed after review B I11)
- Sources read: SKILLSETS ("Traction Splinting of a Extremity Fracture" under "REMOVED in 2024", two openings); JTS-56; SP-TRAC; BSA-WFA (femur care; no traction splint); TCCC26 (no mention); Red Cross Wilderness and Remote First Aid 2014 (no traction splint found).
- Correction: the first record said StatPearls gives "about 10% of body weight" and only a short technique summary. Review B found that StatPearls EMS Traction Splint does give step-by-step Hare and Sager application, with traction of "roughly 10% to 15%" of body weight. StatPearls is a second source only, outside the top tier.
- Decision (controller ruling on review B I11): src/protocols/traction-splint.json removed. Reasons: TCCC dropped traction splinting from its skill sets in 2024; no top-tier source gives the steps; the file taught nothing beyond Broken bone (fracture); as a draft it blocked the release build. fracture.json gained the keywords "traction splint", "sager" and "hare", and its sentence now reads "Traction splints for the thigh are not covered in this app. Binding a pelvis is an advanced procedure: use the Pelvic binder protocol." No other file named a traction-splint protocol by title (grep of src/ and tests/).

## tourniquet-conversion
- Sources read: TCCC26 (6g quotes; Summary of 2026 Changes); SKILLSETS; CLS-09-2024 skill instructions and notes; CMC-TQC-2025; WMS-WOUND.
- Numbers checked:
  - 2 to 3 inches above the bleeding site — repositioning — TCCC26 6g quote; CLS-09-2024; 2026 summary.
  - Less than 2 hours — conversion target — TCCC26 6g quote; CLS-09-2024; CMC-TQC-2025.
  - Not beyond 2 hours for ASM/CLS unless directed by CMC/CPP — TCCC26 6g quote (two openings).
  - More than 6 hours — do not remove unless close monitoring and lab capability — TCCC26 6g quote; CLS-09-2024 ("more than 6 hours"); CLS-09-2024 notes ("six or more hours").
  - One minute — slow release of a tourniquet — CLS-09-2024 (replacement and conversion); CMC-TQC-2025; notes ("at least one minute").
  - At least 3 minutes; 1 to 2 inches — packing — CLS-09-2024; CMC-TQC-2025.
  - 4 minutes — conversion standard — CLS-09-2024 (first reading only).
  - 2 hours / 6 hours — retry window after a failed conversion — CLS-09-2024 notes (first reading only).
- Notes: Three conversion criteria quoted from TCCC26 6g. The loosened tourniquet is left loose in place above the dressing: CLS-09-2024 notes. Leaving the original tight above a replacement risks compartment syndrome: CLS-09-2024 notes. WMS-WOUND (over 2 hours, leave until definitive evaluation) disagrees: open question. Consistent with bleeding s7 and wound-care s11 (leave tourniquets for the trained) and with bleeding's don'ts.

## wound-closure
- Sources read: WMS-WOUND (dtic copy quotes; FEMECV copy); BSA-WFA; MLP-GLUE (two openings).
- Numbers checked:
  - 6 hours; 10 hours face and scalp — closure window — WMS-WOUND both copies (1C).
  - 4 days — closure delay for rabies-prone wounds — WMS-WOUND both copies (1C).
  - 1% to 12% — infection risk with proper care — WMS-WOUND both copies.
  - At least 1 litre — irrigation — WMS-WOUND (as wound-care).
  - Half an inch — gape limit for field closure — BSA-WFA p. 27 (two openings).
  - About 1 minute — hold closed while glue dries; 5 to 10 days — seal lasts; 10 minutes — bleeding despite pressure; 100 F (37.7 C) — fever — MLP-GLUE (two openings).
- Notes: Grades quoted from WMS-WOUND dtic copy: primary closure 1B, contaminated packed open 1B, devitalised left open 1C, abrasions 1C, adhesive for simple wounds 1A. Strips as handles: BSA-WFA p. 23 quote. Hair apposition: WMS-WOUND (validated for scalp: dtic quote; twist hair and secure with adhesive: FEMECV copy, first reading only). Staples never on the face and sutures/staples preferred for complex wounds: WMS-WOUND; technique and anaesthetic not described in the app. Irrigation points to the Wound cleaning and care protocol by title; wound-care s6 sends trained users here.

## dislocation-reduction
- Sources read: BSA-WFA p. 18-20 (two openings); SP-SHLD.
- Numbers checked:
  - 5 to 10 pounds — Stimson weight — BSA-WFA quote (two openings).
  - 20 to 30 minutes — Stimson wait — BSA-WFA quote (two openings).
  - Over 40% — axillary nerve compromise; about 25% — significant fracture; up to 40% — associated injuries — SP-SHLD (first reading only).
- Notes: Joints limited to front shoulder, kneecap, fingers and toes (BSA-WFA). Self-reduction, finger/toe and kneecap techniques, stop if pain increases dramatically, sling and swathe, buddy tape not over the joint, kneecap splint off the kneecap, evacuate first-time dislocations: BSA-WFA quotes. Contraindications (humeral neck fracture, neurovascular compromise, arterial injury, repeated attempts) and Kocher/Hippocratic not recommended: SP-SHLD. Axillary nerve sensation over the outer shoulder: SP-SHLD. Spine screen agrees with fracture s3/s4. The one-attempt rule with poor circulation is a labelled group judgement (open question). Consistent with sprain-dislocation s3 (splint as found unless trained).

## Task 15 path check
Every path through each protocol was listed with a script: npa 14, needle-decompression 48 (including the recurrence loop s12-s14 and the second-site loop), cricothyrotomy 19, hemostatic-packing 36 (one self-loop at s16 "loosen and rewrap until warm"), junctional-bleeding 33, pelvic-binder 6, traction-splint 1, tourniquet-conversion 213, wound-closure 18, dislocation-reduction 32. Each first step asks about hands-on training; every "no" goes to an end step naming the standard protocol by exact title. Screening before the first invasive step: npa (awake and talking; clear fluid; midface injury), needle-decompression (torso trauma or blast; seal burped; sign present), cricothyrotomy (age; injury blockage; positioning failed), hemostatic-packing (site; limb tourniquet first; object stuck), junctional-bleeding (site; object stuck), pelvic-binder (mechanism; indication), tourniquet-conversion (6 hours; amputation; shock; can monitor; 2 hours and who), wound-closure (bleeding control needed; bite; age of wound; contamination; gape; puncture or scrape; clean after irrigation), dislocation-reduction (spine; joint; suspected fracture; circulation). Each has a "did it work?" ask after the key step with an escalating "no" branch, a monitoring step and an evacuation statement. Changes made by the check: dislocation s6 relabelled as group judgement; junctional-bleeding lost a don't that no source stated; cricothyrotomy asks rephrased to one fact each; hemostatic-packing log text on s14 made true for the groin path.

Cross-references (exact titles, checked against src/protocols/): Recovery position; CPR, adult; CPR, child; Choking, adult and child; Head injury and concussion; Neck or back (spine) injury; Trauma check (MARCH); Shock; Open chest wound; Severe bleeding; Belly (abdominal) injury; Impaled object; Broken bone (fracture); Sprain, strain or dislocation; Wound cleaning and care; Animal or human bite (and rabies risk); and the new titles Cricothyroidotomy (surgical airway), Hemostatic gauze wound packing, Junctional bleeding (groin, armpit, neck), Tourniquet reassessment and conversion. Pointers into this batch from existing protocols use "Advanced procedures (needle decompression)" (chest-wound s7, march), "(tourniquet conversion)" (bleeding s7), "(hemostatic gauze)" (bleeding s8), and plain "see Advanced procedures" (sprain-dislocation, wound-care, fracture says traction splints and pelvic binding are not covered there). They match the new titles in substance; none was edited (outside this task). Rules agree: chest-wound burps seals before needle decompression (needle-decompression s4/s5); bleeding presses belly and chest wounds and packs only limb, neck, armpit and groin (hemostatic-packing s3); bleeding s7 and wound-care s11 leave tourniquets for the trained (tourniquet-conversion s1 "no"); fracture s5/s6 keeps hip and pelvis still (pelvic-binder s5); sprain-dislocation s3 splints as found unless trained (dislocation-reduction); wound-care s6 leaves bites, punctures and dirty wounds open (wound-closure s4, s6, s8). The one difference, pulling out a failed hemostatic dressing versus never pulling out plain packing, is an open question.

## Procedures left out
traction-splint was removed after review (see its section above). The other nine are fully written; no drafts remain.

- Source opened: CoTCCC, Combat Medic/Corpsman skill card "9-Line MEDEVAC and MIST Preparation" #TCCC-CMC-SC-60 (14 May 2021), https://learning-media.allogy.com/api/v1/pdf/05f20f69-ca28-4513-8d5a-e6c4ae5228de/contents (opened twice; second time for word-for-word line headings and options). Also read, for comparison only: "Table 1. NATO 9 Line Medical Evacuation (MEDEVAC) Request" (no issuer or date shown), https://learning-media.allogy.com/api/v1/pdf/470a5f61-6984-4c5c-83ba-f030ba17f76c/contents . ATP 4-02.2 was not opened.
- Comparison, app (draft) vs SC-60, and change made:
  - Line 1: "Location of pickup site (MGRS)" vs "Location of the pickup site" (8-digit grid coordinates) → "Location of the pickup site (grid coordinates)".
  - Line 2: "Radio frequency and call sign" vs "Radio frequency, call sign, and suffix" → "Radio frequency, call sign and suffix".
  - Line 3: "Patients by precedence" vs "Numbers of patients by precedence" (NATO table: "Number of patients by precedence") → "Number of patients by precedence". Options A Urgent, B Urgent surgical, C Priority, D Routine, E Convenience: match.
  - Line 4: "Special equipment" vs "Special equipment required" → changed. Options A None, B Hoist, C Extraction equipment, D Ventilator: match.
  - Line 5: "Patients by type" vs "Number of patients by type" → changed. L Litter, A Ambulatory: match.

Options changed to SC-60 wording: N "No enemy troops in the area", P "Possible enemy troops in the area", E "Enemy troops in the area; approach with caution" (was "Enemy troops in area"), X "Enemy troops in area; armed escort required" (was "Armed escort required"). The form now has a peacetime mode with free-text fields for line 6 and line 9 (wording above); wartime keeps the security codes.

NATO table: CBRN with R radiological (open question).

- Review B (2026-11-17): a second TCCC document, CLS Module 19 Skill Instructions #TCCC-CLS-09-01 (25 Jan 2020), https://learning-media.allogy.com/api/v1/pdf/55c3ac67-5e2f-4d38-a7d4-b7243d41a6e1/contents (opened by the author during the fixes), gives: line 1 "Location of the pickup site (8-digit grid coordinate)"; line 3 "Numbers of patients by precedence"; line 8 "Patient nationality and status (encrypt this using brevity codes)" with E "Enemy Prisoner (EPW)"; line 9 wartime "CBRN Contamination (encrypt this using brevity codes)" with N, B, C; line 9 peacetime repeating the line 6 peacetime text (apparently an error). The app keeps SC-60.

- Re-opened: TCCC26 Summary of 2026 Changes (reperfusion injury, recurrent hemorrhage, clot destabilization, delayed recognition of rebleeding); CPP-08-2022 NDC (numbered 1, 15-27); CMC-06-2021 junctional devices (numbered cleanly; "applied after proper wound packing... following steps 1-5"); WMS-WOUND dtic copy (axilla/groin note, 8-12 h / 24 h note, infection "if equipment and training allow", evacuation list, adhesive vs sutures 1A, Table 3 closure by class); SP-SHLD (Stimson 5 to 15 lb, usually within 30 minutes, reduction may be subtle); SC-60 line 9; MedlinePlus not needed.

### Cricothyrotomy step order re-derived (review A C1, C2, I1, I2, I3)
The 2023 cards SC-66/SC-67 are two-column PDFs whose extracted text scrambles the order. The whole sequence was re-derived from CMC-07-2021 instructions (I) and checklist (C):
- s6 position supine, neck neutral, dominant side; equipment assembled and tested — I 02-03; C 3-5.
- s7 locate the membrane in the V between cricoid and thyroid cartilages; clean if time permits — I 04-05; C 6-7.
- s8 stabilise larynx, confirm landmarks with index finger, 1-inch vertical skin incision — I 06-08; C 8-10.
- s8b reconfirm the membrane with the index finger — I 09; C 11.
- s9 scalpel horizontal, puncture membrane, half-inch incision, depth caution; blade stays in — I 10; C 12.
- Bougie: s10a hook through membrane, lift cricoid, withdraw scalpel — I 11; C 13-15. s10 bougie toward lungs, feel rings; angle hook to shoulder and remove — I 12-13; C 16-17. s10c tube over bougie at least a quarter to 1 inch beyond the cuff; remove bougie — I 14-15; C 18-19.
- Open: s11 Kelly hemostat if available; hook through membrane, lift cricoid, withdraw scalpel — I 11-12; C 13-16. s12 tube toward lungs; angle hook to shoulder and remove; stylet out (ET tube a quarter to 1 inch beyond cuff) — I 13-15; C 17-19.
- s14 cuff 10 mL, misting; helper ventilates — I 16-17; C 20-22.
- s15 auscultate both lungs, equal chest rise — I 18; C 23.
- s16/s16a right side only: deflate cuff, pull back a few centimetres, reinflate — I 18 note.
- s18 spontaneous breathing at least 10 s, oximeter; BVM if below 8 or above 30 or SpO2 below 90% — I 19-20; C 24-25.
- s17 dressing, secure — I 21-22; C 26-27 (now after s18, as the source orders it).
- s19 monitor — I 23; C 28.
- s13 and s25: no source step (no failure procedure); s25 labelled group judgement.
- Condition and standard (s1 detail): used when manual methods, an NPA or extraglottic airways cannot maintain the airway; effective airway in 4 minutes or less — I condition and standard lines.

### Other two-column cards checked for the same extraction problem
- npa: re-derived from CMC-07-2021 instructions 01-10 and checklist 1-13: supine neutral, inspect, open, lubricate, expose right nostril, bevel to septum, insert at 90 degrees to flange, (fail: left nostril, rotate after about 2 inches), reassess, position. The app order (s6 to s14) matches. The 2025 card SC-29's order (expose before lubricate) was an extraction artefact.
- needle-decompression: CPP-08-2022 skill instructions (numbered 1, 15-27, coherent): assess, burp seal, site, secure unit, clean, insert, hold and remove needle, assess success, second NDC at other site, position injured side down, reassess, repeat at same site if recurs, then circulation. TCCC26 5a gives positioning before decompression. The app order matches; the job aid SC-26's order (insert before assess) was an extraction artefact.
- junctional-bleeding devices: CMC-06-2021 numbered 01-12 (SAM), 01-19 (CRoC), 01-18 (JETT), clean. The device details in s11 follow these orders.
- pelvic-binder: re-derived from CPP-09-2026 instructions 1-10 and checklist 1-11. The card SC-38 had shown "pull until click" before "strap through buckle"; the numbered source is: strap through the buckle and pulled completely through (5), opposing straps pulled until the click (6), keep tension and press the free end down (7), legs together (8), monitor for shock (9). pelvic-binder s7 was corrected to this order.
- tourniquet reposition and conversion: CPP-09-2026 numbered 1-10 (reposition) and 1-12 (conversion), and checklists, match the CLS 2024 order used in the app. SC-37 (2026) is two-column and scrambled; it is cited for currency, the CPP 2026 instructions for order.

- C1, C2, I3: cric order as above. C3: npa s3b "Is the casualty unconscious or only partly responsive?"; no goes to s23 (TCCC 4c: let them take the position that best protects the airway, including sitting up or leaning forward; no NPA; watch the airway; evacuate urgently; come back if they become unresponsive). Indication now names the CMC 2021 condition (unconscious or semiconscious).
- I1: s16a deflate, pull back a few centimetres, reinflate (CMC-07-2021 step 18 note). I2: s16 is an ask (right side only?); no goes to s25, labelled group judgement with the controller's wording; open question updated.
- I4: hemostatic-packing s5 now "Is a tourniquet already on this limb?"; yes goes to s5b (tighten, second tourniquet side by side, TCCC 2026 3a), and packing only if still bleeding. I5: s15a skips the pulse check when a tourniquet is on the limb; s16 no goes to s16x (group judgement, open question). I6: junctional-bleeding asks about the groin right after packing; device applied as soon as ready with a helper readying it; 3-minute hold for no device and for neck and armpit; summary and indications updated. I7: hemostatic-packing s33 now matches Impaled object and junctional s32. I8: npa contraindications and don'ts attributed "(StatPearls; current TCCC lists none)"; open question reworded.
- M1 NDC indication numbers moved to distress; M2 signs listed in s5 detail; M3 "Right side first" dropped, logs say first and second side; M4 s31 adds CPR; M5 "TCCC specifies only this size"; M6 StatPearls attributed to chest tube insertion; M7 neck airway warning attributed to TCCC 2026 after a neck clamp, the wider use labelled the app's caution (hemostatic-packing s14, junctional s26 and complications); M8 hemostatic s12 split groin vs armpit/neck; M9 forehead for the improvised device labelled the app's choice; M10 cric s4 "deformity", s32 adds Anaphylaxis (severe allergic reaction); M11 cric condition and standard in s1; M12 "petroleum-based"; M13 bleeding s7/s8, chest-wound s7 and don't, march s7/s16 now name "Needle decompression of the chest", "Tourniquet reassessment and conversion" and "Hemostatic gauze wound packing" (wording only); M14 tiers: reviewer A confirmed them against JSOM 2019 p.145 (not opened by the author); M15 no change.

- I1: tourniquet-conversion s3 and s5 now ask whether bleeding / the pulse is controlled; no goes to s41 (leave both tight, no reposition or conversion, evacuate urgently). I2: s26 asks "Is the bleeding controlled now?" to s26y (leave it, no second conversion, group judgement) or s26n; s27 "evacuate urgently". - I7: wound-closure s6 adds armpit and groin (labelled app's caution, since WMS still closes dirty wounds primarily); new s8b screens numbness or weakness, joint involvement and possible fracture. I8: s13s asks for strips when glue cannot be used; no goes to leave open. I9: s18 action "if trained and equipped, remove the strips or glue so it can drain; otherwise leave it covered. Evacuate (urgently with fever, confusion or a red streak)."
- I10: dislocation-reduction restructured (see the updated open question); Red Cross WRFA is now the lead source; post-reduction question is "still pale, blue, cold or numb, or worse than before?", yes to rapid evacuation.
- I11: traction-splint removed (see its section).
- Minors: timed asks say "After the full minute"; summary says "aim: within 2 hours"; indications note that removal of an unneeded tourniquet is not guided; complications add reperfusion injury, recurrent haemorrhage, clot destabilisation, delayed recognition of rebleeding; s18a asks wound suitability; SC-37 (2026) and CPP-09-2026 cited; s26 names Hemostatic gauze wound packing; pelvic s4 lists lay-visible signs only, palpation left to trained examiners; "front points of the hip bones (anterior superior iliac spine)" replaces "hip-bone crests", "(not the lower back)" dropped; s5 airway-first if vomiting; dislocation s10 grip clause dropped; s11 detail carries the success description; "soft cloth" removed, 5 to 10 lb attributed to Red Cross and BSA with the StatPearls 5 to 15 lb difference recorded; "slips back noticeably" replaced by StatPearls "may be subtle"; spine screen narrowed (fingers and toes done where they lie); s14 logs "Reduction attempted, not successful"; wound-closure s10 detail gives the WMS preference order (glue for simple wounds, strips weakest) and s5 records WMS Table 3's 8-12 h / 24 h; 9-line label as above.

### Path check after fixes
Paths listed by script: npa 15, needle-decompression 48, cricothyrotomy 23, hemostatic-packing 45, junctional-bleeding 48, pelvic-binder 6, tourniquet-conversion 268, wound-closure 20, dislocation-reduction 61; touched protocols bleeding 6, chest-wound 4, march 34, fracture 9. No path falls off the end without "end"; the only loops are the intended NDC recurrence loop and the hemostatic "loosen and rewrap" loop, which now has an exit after one rewrap. Cross-references in both directions re-checked: chest-wound and march name Needle decompression of the chest; bleeding names Tourniquet reassessment and conversion and Hemostatic gauze wound packing; fracture names Pelvic binder; wound-care and sprain-dislocation still say "see Advanced procedures" (not in the controller's edit list; titles exist in the Advanced procedures category). Rules agree: chest wounds burp before decompression; belly and chest wounds are pressed, not packed; tourniquets stay on for the untrained (bleeding, wound-care, tourniquet-conversion s30, Red Cross WRFA); hip and pelvis kept still (fracture, pelvic-binder); dislocations splinted as found unless trained (sprain-dislocation); impaled objects pressed around and padded, never removed (impaled-object, hemostatic-packing s33, junctional s32).

## Daylight and moon reference values

Task 10. `tests/fixtures/almanac-syracuse.json` holds the reference values that `tests/test_astro.py` checks
`FK_ASTRO` against: sun times within 2 minutes, moon times within 5 minutes, illuminated fraction within 3 percentage
points, and phase names by the rule below. `tests/test_survival_tools.py` also checks the Daylight and moon page's
cards against the same rows.

**Source.** US Naval Observatory, Astronomical Applications Department, "Complete Sun and Moon Data for One Day",
one page per row:
`https://aa.usno.navy.mil/calculated/rstt/oneday?date=YYYY-MM-DD&lat=LAT&lon=LON&label=NAME&tz=H&tz_sign=-1&tz_label=false&dst=true|false&submit=Get+Data`.
The controller fetched every row on 2026-11-17, after the user approved the fetch. The values are recorded in
`.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-17-v7-survival-framework/usno-fetched.md`.
- Syracuse, NY (43.05 N, 76.15 W): 2026-03-07, 03-08, 03-09, 03-20, 06-21, 09-22, 10-31, 11-01, 11-02, 11-09, 11-17,
  11-24 and 12-21. Each row has civil twilight begin and end, sunrise, sunset, moonrise and moonset ("none listed"
  is stored as null), the phase text with percent illuminated, and the closest primary phase.
- Utqiagvik, AK (71.29 N, 156.79 W): 2026-06-21, sun "Object continuously above the Horizon" and "continuously above
  the Twilight Limit", moonset 02:09, moonrise 15:00, First Quarter 50%. 2026-12-21, sun "Object continuously below
  the Horizon", civil twilight begins 11:56 and ends 14:55, moon "Object continuously above the Horizon", Waxing
  Gibbous 93%.
- **Times are local wall-clock times as the page printed them.** On 2026-03-08 they are EDT. On 2026-11-01 they are
  EST after the 2 a.m. change (sunrise 06:39), so they are the times a phone in America/New_York shows. The tests
  turn each one into an instant with the browser's own America/New_York or America/Anchorage rules.
- **Two oddities on the pages:**
  - The 31 October page gives last quarter as 16:28 EDT, and the 1 and 2 November pages give 15:28 EST. This is
    the same instant.
  - The 1 November moonset of 13:22 is EST, which is 14:22 EDT, so it fits the usual daily delay.
- **Phase names.** USNO's daily phase text names a primary phase on some days it happens, but not on others:
  - It does name New Moon on 11-09, Full Moon on 11-24, and First Quarter on 06-21 at Utqiagvik.
  - It does not on 11-17, which is the First Quarter day (06:48) but is printed "Waxing Gibbous with 52%".
  - It does not on 06-21 at Syracuse ("Waxing Crescent, 48%"), nor on 11-01, the Last Quarter day ("Waning Gibbous,
    52%").

  The app names a primary phase on the local day it happens. The test's tolerance: the app's name must equal USNO's
  text, or, on the local date of USNO's closest primary phase, equal that primary phase.

**Discarded: the USNO JSON API.** `https://aa.usno.navy.mil/api/rstt/oneday?...` was also fetched for every date.
The fetch tool returned the 2026-06-21 Syracuse answer for every query, from a cache keyed on the path, so those
answers were thrown away. Only the "calculated" page values are used. For 2026-06-21 Syracuse, the API answer and the
page agree.

**Dropped: the earlier sources.** These were opened with WebFetch on 2026-11-17 before USNO could be fetched. None
of their values is in the fixture now.
- sunrisesunset.io (`/us/new-york/syracuse/2026/`), for sun times.
  - Its March and June rows were 2 to 3.4 minutes from USNO, in the direction of a longer day: 20 March sunrise 7:05
    against USNO 07:08, and 21 June sunset 8:50 PM against USNO 20:48.
  - Its September to December rows were within a minute of USNO.
- sunrise-sunset.org, for Syracuse in November 2026 and Utqiagvik in November 2026.
- time-ok.com (`/moon/syracuse`), for moon times and phases.
  - Its moonrise and moonset times were 15 to 24 minutes from USNO. For example, 1 November moonrise was 10:53 PM
    against USNO 23:17.
  - Its November phase days agree with USNO.
- Washington Times (11 May 2026) and Fox Weather (18 November 2025), news reports of midnight sun and polar night at
  Utqiagvik.

An intermediate version of the fixture labelled Utqiagvik 2026-12-21 as a geometric inference. That row now has
USNO's own "continuously below the Horizon".

## Task 13: Survival topics, water and priorities (2026-11-17)

Source keys (all opened 2026-11-17 by web fetch; title checked after each fetch):
- EPA-ED: US EPA, Emergency Disinfection of Drinking Water. https://www.epa.gov/ground-water-and-drinking-water/emergency-disinfection-drinking-water
- CDC-MWS: CDC, How to Make Water Safe in an Emergency. https://cdc.gov/water-emergency/about
- CDC-FCW: CDC, How to Find Clean Water in an Emergency. https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/how-to-find-clean-water-in-an-emergency.html
- NYSDOH-HAB: New York State Department of Health, Harmful Blue-Green Algae Blooms. https://healthweb-back.health.ny.gov/environmental/water/drinking/bluegreenalgae/ (the page itself does not print its publisher; the health.ny.gov domain is NYS DOH)
- ATP: US Army ATP 3-50.21, Survival, September 2018, chapter 3 (paragraph numbers below). https://irp.fas.org/doddir/army/atp3-50-21.pdf
- FM1: FM 21-76-1 / MCRP 3-02H / NWP 3-50.3 / AFTTP(I) 3-2.26, Survival, Evasion, and Recovery, 29 June 1999 (page numbers below). https://rmp.nc.gov/LCF/Publications/AL_Resources/Survival/Multi Service Survival Manual.pdf
- FM21-76: US Army FM 21-76, Survival Manual (2006 PDF copy, chapter 6 text; figures 6-1 and 6-2 are images and did not extract). https://northernbush.com/wp-content/downloads/woodcraft/US-Army-Survival-Manual.pdf and https://www.sjsu.edu/people/michelle.hager/docs/fm_21-76_us_army_survival_manual_2006.pdf
- PHX: City of Phoenix Fire Department, Desert survival (page prints no publisher; phoenix.gov fire department section). https://phoenix.gov/fire/safety-information/outdoor/desert-survival
- TOOELE: Tooele County Health Department (Utah), Rule of Three. https://tooelehealth.org/?p=4919 and the PDF https://tooelehealth.org/wp-content/uploads/2016/06/Rule-of-3-Emerg.-Plan-English.pdf
- DEC-LOST: NYS DEC, Lost in the woods leaflet. https://extapps.dec.ny.gov/docs/legal_protection_pdf/lostinwoods.pdf
- DEC-CAMP: NYS DEC, State Land Camping Rules. https://dec.ny.gov/things-to-do/camping/state-land-rules
- NPS-OEP: National Park Service, Outdoor Emergency Plan (PDF). https://www.nps.gov/subjects/healthandsafety/upload/NPS-Outdoor-Emergency-Plan-508c.pdf
- AK-HYPO: Alaska State Parks (Alaska DNR), Hypothermia. https://dnr.alaska.gov/parks/safety/hypother.htm
- USFS-HYPO: US Forest Service, Hypothermia. https://www.fs.usda.gov/visit/know-before-you-go/hypothermia
- CDC-TICK: CDC, Preventing Tick Bites. https://cdc.gov/ticks/prevention/index.html
- ARMY-COLD: army.mil, Fight the Freeze (B. Minchhoff, Fort Drum, 3 Oct 2011). https://www.army.mil/article/66527/fight_the_freeze
- CPSC: US CPSC, Portable Heaters & Camping Equipment. https://www.cpsc.gov/es/node/12866 (English content)
- NOAA-SW: NOAA National Ocean Service, Can humans drink seawater? https://oceanservice.noaa.gov/facts/drinksw.html
- FDA-SEA: FDA, Selecting and Serving Fresh and Frozen Seafood Safely. https://www.fda.gov/food/buy-store-serve-safe-food/selecting-and-serving-fresh-and-frozen-seafood-safely
- MA-PSP: Massachusetts DPH, Red Tide (Paralytic Shellfish Poisoning). https://www.mass.gov/info-details/red-tide-paralytic-shellfish-poisoning
- NPS-TIDE: National Park Service, How Tides Work at Acadia. https://www.nps.gov/articles/000/tides-acadia.htm
- EPA-SUN: US EPA, Sun Safety Tips. https://www.epa.gov/sunsafety/action-steps-sun-safety

### surv-make-water-safe

- Settle, then filter through clean cloth, paper towel or coffee filter — EPA-ED ("let it settle and filter it through a clean cloth, paper towel, or coffee filter"); CDC-MWS. Corrected (added paper towel, coffee filter).
- "Boiling is the best way to kill germs in water" — CDC-MWS verbatim. Replaces the removed figure caption "Boiling is the most reliable method".
- Rolling boil at least 1 minute — EPA-ED ("rolling boil for at least one minute"); CDC-MWS (1 minute); ATP 3-41 (1 minute). Matches.
- 3 minutes above 5,000 ft — EPA-ED ("above 5,000 feet (1,000 meters), boil water for three minutes"). CDC-MWS: above 6,500 feet. ATP 3-41: above 8,000 feet. Old text said 6,500 ft. Corrected to the most cautious (EPA). Feet only: EPA's metric figure (1,000 m) does not equal 5,000 ft, so no metric is given.
- Boil time disagreement — NYS DEC State Land Camping Rules: boil 5 minutes; ATP 2-3 and FM1 chapter V also say 5 minutes. EPA and CDC (the water-treatment authorities named for this task) say 1 minute. The text follows EPA/CDC. Open question added to docs/decisions-for-jordan.md.
- Bleach type: regular unscented chlorine bleach; not scented, color-safe or with added cleaners; 6 or 8.25% — EPA-ED. Corrected (added).
- Bleach age: stored at room temperature for less than one year — EPA-ED. Matches the old caption's "not expired", made specific.
- 8 drops per gallon of 6% — EPA-ED table; CDC-MWS (8 drops for 5% to 9%). Matches.
- 8.25% bleach: old text 6 drops per gallon — EPA-ED table gives 6 drops per gallon for 8.25%; CDC-MWS gives 8 drops per gallon for any 5% to 9%. Sources disagree; the larger dose (CDC) is used for both strengths. Corrected. Open question added.
- 2 drops per quart or liter — EPA-ED (both strengths); CDC-MWS (5% to 9%). Added.
- Double for cloudy, colored or very cold — EPA-ED; CDC-MWS ("cloudy, murky, colored, or very cold"). Old text "cloudy or very cold": corrected (colored added).
- Stir and stand 30 minutes — EPA-ED; CDC-MWS ("at least 30 minutes"). Matches.
- Slight chlorine smell; if none, repeat dose and stand another 15 minutes — EPA-ED. Added.
- Tablets: follow the label, products differ in strength — EPA-ED. Matches.
- Iodine and chlorine tablets do not kill Cryptosporidium; chlorine dioxide does if instructions followed — CDC-MWS. ATP 3-36 note agrees that chemical disinfectants are not fully effective against Cryptosporidium. Added.
- Iodine: avoid if pregnant or thyroid problems; no one more than a few weeks — CDC-MWS. Added (CDC also lists iodine hypersensitivity; not added to keep the step short — consider for pack 1).
- Portable filters do not remove viruses; disinfect after filtering — CDC-MWS ("Will not remove viruses"). ATP 3-45 says "most filters do not remove viruses". Old text "Most filters don't stop viruses": corrected to CDC (more cautious).
- Fuel, toxic chemicals, radioactive material cannot be made safe by boiling or disinfecting — CDC-MWS verbatim meaning; CDC-FCW. Added.
- Heavy metals, salts, most other chemicals not destroyed by boiling or disinfection — EPA-ED. Added.
- Harmful algal blooms: appearance (strongly colored water in the listed colors, paint-like, floating mats or scums); do not drink, prepare food or make ice with surface water during a bloom even if treated; boiling does not remove the algae or toxins — NYSDOH-HAB. Added. No opened source says what home or camping filters do for bloom toxins, so filters are covered only by "even if you treat it yourself".
- Figure `water-safe`: removed (drawn text says 3 minutes above 6,500 ft, wrong against the corrected text). Figure file left in figs/.
- Solar disinfection, "clear water is safe": not in the topic; nothing to check. Not added (FM1 VII-4 mentions sun disinfection without a time; adding it would expand the topic and needs a time from CDC or WHO).
- Tags: none set (applies everywhere). Unchanged.

### surv-find-water

- Water runs downhill; valley bottoms and low ground — FM1 VII-1 ("Drainages and low-lying areas"). Matches.
- Foot of cliffs or rocks — ATP 3-34 ("the foot of cliffs or rocks"); PHX ("at the base of rock cliffs"). Matches.
- Dry stream beds, dig on the outside of bends — ATP 3-34 ("Outside bends of a dry riverbed"); PHX ("digging three to six feet at the outside edge of a sharp bend in a dry stream bed"). Matches. The depth is not added.
- `water-bend` caption: wet sand, dig into it for seeping water — PHX; ATP Table 3-1 (dig deep enough for water to seep in). Corrected; old explanation removed-unsourced. Figure kept (matches).
- Signs: lush green vegetation, swarming insects, V-intersecting game trails, bird flight in early morning or late afternoon may indicate the direction — FM1 VII-1. Corrected ("low and straight at dawn and dusk" not found in any opened source; the FM 21-76 figure that may hold it did not extract).
- Rain on a tarp, poncho or leaves — ATP 3-11 (improvised nonporous items), ATP 3-10 (rain off leaves should be purified). Matches.
- Rain needs treatment — CDC-FCW (rainwater is a source "that could be made safe by treatment"; outside water "must be treated"). ATP 3-10: fresh rain collected into containers needs no purification. Sources disagree; CDC (more cautious) used: "Treat all wild water, rain included".
- Dew: cloth around ankles before sunrise, walk through grass, wring out — FM21-76 chapter 6 ("Tie rags or tufts of fine grass around your ankles and walk through dew-covered grass before sunrise"); ATP 3-15. Matches.
- Transpiration bag: clear bag, non-poisonous plant, tied over a leafy branch and sealed — FM1 VII-6 (clear; no poisonous/toxic plants); ATP 3-27 (sealed airtight). Corrected ("and seal it" added).
- `water-bag` caption: collect before sundown; same limb 3 or 5 days; water tastes like the plant — ATP 3-27; FM1 VII-6. Corrected ("move it to a fresh branch daily" contradicted ATP; "a little water over several hours" unsourced). Figure kept (matches).
- Never drink seawater or urine — ATP 3-2; FM1 VII-1. Matches.
- Harmful algal blooms bullet — NYSDOH-HAB. Added.
- Tags: none set. Unchanged.

### surv-priorities

- Rule of threes — TOOELE ("about three minutes in unsafe air", "about three hours at a dangerous temperature", "about three days without water", "about three weeks without food"; "Although it is not absolute, the rule of three gives good general direction"). Kept as a rough guide; "without shelter" changed to "at a dangerous temperature" to match. No federal or military source opened states the rule (FM1 and ATP were checked: not found). County health department is below the preferred tier; noted as a concern.
- STOP — DEC-LOST: "S is for Sit Down", Think, Observe, Plan. Corrected (old: Stop).
- Stay put if lost — NPS-OEP ("Stay put – Changing location will make it difficult for authorities to find you"; move only if life is under immediate threat; leave a trip plan with a trusted contact). Corrected.
- Hypothermia above freezing — DEC-LOST. Most cases 30 to 50°F — AK-HYPO ("Most cases occur when temperatures are between 30 and 50 degrees F"); wind and wet clothes — AK-HYPO, USFS-HYPO. Corrected (superlative removed).
- Treat all stream, pond and lake water — CDC-FCW; DEC-LOST ("filter or treat"). Matches; "usually plentiful" removed-unsourced.
- Dead and down wood only; cutting standing trees prohibited on state land — DEC-CAMP. Corrected; "fire usually achievable" and "standing dead wood" removed-unsourced. This is the normal-times rule; whether it should yield in a real emergency is for the Fire topic (Task 14) and Jordan.
- Tick check, body areas — CDC-TICK. Corrected ("every day" not in the source).
- COLD — ARMY-COLD (Clean, avoid Overheating, Loose and Layered, Dry); FM1 (clean, dry, loose and layered, remove layers before strenuous activity); AK-HYPO (remove layers when you start to sweat). Matches; explanation reworded.
- Cover head, hands and feet — FM1 (hat regulates body heat); ARMY-COLD (layering applies to boots, gloves, headgear); DEC-LOST (hat covering ears). Matches.
- Eat and drink regularly, fuel to make heat — USFS-HYPO ("Water helps turn calories into heat and food high in carbohydrates helps keep you powered"). Matches.
- Don't eat snow or ice, lowers body temperature; melt it — FM1 VII-1, VII-3. Treat melted snow — ATP 3-13, Table 3-1. Corrected.
- No stove, heater or lantern while sleeping in a tent — CPSC; air vent for any flame in an enclosed shelter — FM1 VI-4; people die every year — CPSC. Corrected. Consistent with the carbon-monoxide protocol s6.
- Sunglasses; thin horizontal slits in bark or cloth — FM1 VI-3 ("small horizontal slits in webbing, bark, or similar materials"); snow blindness — FM1 V-10. Matches ("horizontal" added).
- Never drink seawater, deadly, dehydration — NOAA-SW. Corrected wording.
- Beach well behind the first group of dunes, deep enough to hit the water table — ATP 3-33, Table 3-1; treat — CDC-FCW. Corrected.
- Shellfish: cracked shells, tap test, discard unopened after cooking — FDA-SEA; closed areas, cooking does not destroy red tide toxin, toxic without discolored water — MA-PSP. Corrected. (MA-PSP also lists oysters, whelks and moon snails as affected; not added.)
- Tide chart; land bridge trap; Acadia range about 12 feet — NPS-TIDE. Corrected. No metric given (the topic has none elsewhere).
- Water and sand reflect the sun, increase sunburn chance; cover up — EPA-SUN. Matches.
- Links: `p:frostbite` kept; added `p:hypothermia` (Forest), `p:tick` (Forest), `p:carbon-monoxide` (Cold and snow). `p:dehydration` and `p:heat-illness` not linked: the topic restates no dehydration or heat-illness treatment.
- Tags: none set at topic level (sections cover several settings). Unchanged.
